unsafe identifiers are rejected pre-proxy and valid UUIDv4 identifiers may reach a configured
stateful backend before the unavailable fallback is selected.

The frontend projection, production-parser and proxy-boundary suite passed `5/5`; Worker

instead of trusting free-form deployment IDs or booleans.

## Historical RC21 And Follow-up Verification

Recorded 2026-08-29. The active candidate
`prod-candidate-2026-08-28-local-rc21` is bound to source
`c1b022a884eb16939fe0542b2eb9056b60706b20` through control
`9f2ee3838492079bd5c65b53a03cd4b29c9a6c49` and source-attested GitHub Actions run
`33217980790`. Its committed five-chain evidence and CI readback prove the six local images,
runtime, real Chromium browser (`22/22`, `29/29`, `161/161`), candidate-runtime identity and
security chain. `npm run verify:phase5-credit` reports `verified 17/19 = 89%` with only I1
and I5 blocked. `npm run verify:current-release-candidate` reports
`candidate_technical=true`, `runtime_source_parity=true`, `promotion_eligible=false` and
canonical `blocked`, which is the expected non-production result.

The CI completeness audit first found two skipped Phase-5 steps in run `33217980790`.
After replacing three mutually exclusive workflow steps with one always-running mode router,
run `33223542872` verified head
`e98f68a6e5ce8544f8504f38a57c0e17672fe253`: one job, `25/25` successful steps,
`0` skipped and no non-success conclusion. The run includes the generated-HTML guard, OAuth
boundary, stateful-runtime and LLM-gateway contracts, gitleaks and all Phase-1 image builds.

The local `github_actions=api_error` was a stale container-environment defect, not an expired
Owner credential: the DEV-ONLY container token differed from the matching local secret-file
and authenticated `gh` keyring token, while `gh api rate_limit` was healthy. A scoped
`agent-api` recreation restored the provider inventory to `configured=true`,
`live_verified=true`, `status=verified`, three resources and no error. No token value was
emitted and no rotation occurred.

The Owner console configuration was API/UI-read back: both GitHub publication environments
require reviewer `strazzusochr`, and Worker secret names are stored as `secret_text`.
Session16 later proved that the recorded Worker OAuth callback and the same URI across
Production/Preview are incompatible with the frontend Same-Origin and `__Host-` cookie
contract. The callback must use a canonical browser-visible frontend origin; unique Preview
origins require a stable staging origin or separate OAuth App. Console configuration remains
preparation only. A current hosted deployment and the full OAuth session/replay proof are
absent, so `production_auth_identity` and I5 remain blocked.

The new `docs/runtime-contracts/layer-credit-rubric.md` is explicitly
`DRAFT_OWNER_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`. Proposed L4 and L5 sums are each 100, but it grants no
credit. Manifest values remain Overall `89`, L4 `55`, L5 `56`, and
`MARKET_READY:false`.

No GHCR publication, production deploy, default-branch push, release promotion, Phase-6
hosted-write run, payment, secret output or visual completion claim was made.

## Current Branch-Protection Safety

`scripts/apply_github_branch_protection.py` now requires explicit `--apply` plus
an available token before it can issue the GitHub `PUT`. A configured token
without `--apply` selects read-only verification; no token selects dry-run.
`.github/workflows/branch-protection.yml` uses the explicit apply form.

Correction recorded 2026-07-31: the script also hard-coded `main` as its default
branch, but `main` does not exist in this repository at all. The default branch is
`chore/repo-bootstrap`, so every live call resolved to an opaque
`404 Branch not found` and the protection state was reported as missing when it was
not. `resolve_target_branch()` now reads the real default branch from the API
whenever a token is present, `assert_branch_exists()` fails closed before any
protection claim and names the actual default branch, and `request_json` accepts
`missing_ok` so "does not exist" is distinguishable from a genuine failure. Two
additional offline self-tests cover explicit-branch precedence and the token-free
fallback.

Read-only verification of the real default branch on 2026-07-31 returned
`status: verified`, `0` mismatches, `allow_force_pushes=false`,
`allow_deletions=false`, `required_approving_review_count=1`, exit `0`. No live
GitHub write was executed. The Owner runbook step that instructed an apply against
`main` has been corrected to state that nothing needs to be applied.

## Historical Branch-Protection External Gate Closure

Recorded 2026-07-31. `github_branch_protection_current_verify` was listed as a
missing external gate, but the protection itself was present the whole time. The
probe in `verify-external-gates.ps1` defaulted `$Branch` to an empty string, so it
requested `https://api.github.com/repos/<repo>/branches/` with no branch and
reported "protection not enabled" for a branch it never asked about.
`Resolve-DefaultBranchName()` now reads the real default branch from the public
repository metadata endpoint, which keeps the token-free bootstrap intact, and
leaves `$Branch` empty so the probe still blocks if the name cannot be resolved.

`docs/runtime-state/external-gate-summary.json` now reports
`missing_or_failed_gates = ghcr_image_digest_verify` with
`branch_protection_claim_allowed = true`, `status` still `blocked` and
`production_deploy_claim_allowed` still `false`. No GitHub write was performed; the
protection state was only read.

The coupled assertions were changed in the same slice. `verify-phase1.ps1` no
longer hard-codes a count of two missing gates: it binds to
`branch_protection_claim_allowed` and fails closed in both directions, so a gate
that is claimed verified may not remain listed, and a gate that is not claimed
verified must remain listed. `verify-market-ready.ps1` was updated in both the
summary and owner-manifest branches. `verify-go-live-readiness.ps1` needed no
change because it was already written conditionally.

Operational note: each `verify-external-gates` run writes a new timestamped
artefact into the git-ignored `.phase1-artifacts/`, and
`verify-retired-hosted-boundary.ps1` requires `PROJECT_STATE.md`, `AI_HANDOFF.md`
and this register to reference the newest one. Those three references must be
updated in the same slice as any gate run.

Superseded 2026-08-29: the current RC22-selector readback no longer proves the
Branch-Protection gate. Current truth is `branch_protection_claim_allowed=false` with
exactly `github_branch_protection_current_verify` and `ghcr_image_digest_verify` missing.
The 2026-07-31 read remains historical proof of the then-observed repository setting only.

## Current Hosted-Acceptance Guard Alignment

Recorded 2026-07-31. `verify-market-ready.ps1` required
`product_acceptance_hosted_proof` and `workspace_22_page_hosted_proof` to be `true`
in `docs/runtime-state/cloudflare-native-hosted-current.json`, while
`verify-phase1.ps1` still required both to be `false` from an earlier era in which
neither hosted acceptance report existed. HEAD `0a982c2a` was therefore red. The
blanket prohibition in `verify-phase1.ps1` is replaced by an evidence-bound guard:
an unclaimed proof must carry no evidence, source, or deployment fields, and a
claimed proof must supply an existing artefact whose SHA-256 matches the recorded
value, a 40-character source commit that is an ancestor of HEAD, a deployment id,
an https base url, and a report that does not contradict the truth file. This is
strictly stronger than the previous guard, which validated no hashes at all.
Negative proof: flipping a single hex character of the recorded evidence hash makes
the verifier reject with `evidence SHA-256 does not match its recorded value`; the
state file was restored byte-exactly afterwards. `verify-phase1.ps1` returned exit
`0` with `gitleaks` reporting no leaks across 3737 files.

`AI_HANDOFF.md` had lost the contract-origin sentence pinned by
`verify-retired-hosted-boundary.ps1`; it was restored alongside the newer hosted
statement, because the Vercel frontend and stateless read-only Backend Contract
Origin remain deployed and the hosted proofs run against exactly those origins.

Known limitation, not introduced here: `verify-live-llm-evidence-chain.ps1`
compares two untracked, git-ignored artefacts and fails hard when they are absent,
so `npm run verify` cannot pass on a fresh clone. The two artefacts had drifted
apart (D1 re-seeded 2026-07-30, browser artefact from 2026-07-25) and were
resynchronised; the chain reports `new_provider_calls=0`, so no percentage credit
arises from it.

## Status Values

- `verified`: inspected and consistent with PATCHED.
- `patched`: corrected during the drift repair.
- `superseded`: kept only for history.
- `retired`: historical blocker preserved, no longer active for the current clean release boundary.
- `pending-runtime-proof`: implementation still needs command/test evidence.
- `blocked`: cannot be completed without a gate.

- `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts\verify-hosted-staging.ps1 -BaseUrl <local-control-plane-url> -AllowLocalhost`: passed after the runtime verifier finished recreating Nginx; the earlier parallel run failed only because Nginx was being recreated and returned HTTP `000`.
- Infra budget proof: `GET /api/v1/infra/budget` returns `projected_cost_cents=900`, `budget_limit_cents=2000`, `warning_limit_cents=1600`, `level=ok`, `allow_new_infra=true`, `source=configured_phase1_projection`, `live_verified=false`, and configured items including `hetzner-production-cx21`; this is a configured Phase-1 projection, not a live Hetzner invoice claim.
- Infra budget UI proof: frontend root contains `Infra Budget`; dashboard fetches `/api/v1/infra/budget` and renders projected spend, hard limit, usage percentage, gate state, itemized monthly cost lines, source, and live-verification status.
- Infra budget metrics proof: `GET /api/v1/metrics` exposes `superbrain_infra_budget_spent_percentage`, `superbrain_infra_budget_projected_cost_cents`, and `superbrain_infra_budget_allow_new`.
- External gates proof: `GET /api/v1/external-gates` returns `status=action_required`, `local_execution_allowed=true`, configured count, total count, and explicit gate records for `branch_protection_token`, `staging_base_url`, `hetzner_api_token`, and `gitleaks_binary`; this makes missing external tokens visible without blocking local execution or claiming hosted/production proof.
- External Gates UI proof: frontend root contains `External Gates`; dashboard fetches `/api/v1/external-gates` and renders configured/missing state plus fallback behavior per gate.
- External gates metrics proof: `GET /api/v1/metrics` exposes `superbrain_external_gate_configured` grouped by gate id and action-required status.
- Resource measurement proof: `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts\measure-compose-resources.ps1 -JsonOnly` returns all eight running Compose services with CPU percent, memory usage, memory percent, memory limits, CPU limits, and the no-upgrade-without-evidence rule; the latest observed frontend container was near its 512MiB limit, so this is logged as resource pressure evidence, not as a server-upgrade approval.
- Frontend production-runtime proof: replacing `next dev` with a multi-stage `next build` + `next start` runtime reduced observed frontend memory from about `461MiB / 512MiB` (`90%`) to about `64MiB / 512MiB` (`12.5%`), removed runtime TypeScript installation, and produced a healthy container with `Ready in 911ms`; runtime and hosted verifiers passed after the change.
- Non-root runtime proof: Docker inspect reports `USER=appuser` for `agent-api`, `agent-worker`, `memory-worker`, `mcp-gateway`, and `frontend`; in-container `id` checks report UID `10001` instead of root, and `scripts\verify-phase1-runtime.ps1` now fails closed if any app container runs as root.
- Container hardening proof: Docker inspect reports `Privileged=false`, `ReadonlyRootfs=true`, `CapDrop=[ALL]`, and `SecurityOpt=[no-new-privileges:true]` for all five app containers; `scripts\verify-phase1-runtime.ps1` and `scripts\verify-phase1.ps1` now fail closed if these settings disappear.
- PostgreSQL backup proof: `scripts\backup-postgres-phase1.ps1` produces a schema-only `pg_dump` plus manifest in `.phase1-artifacts/postgres-backups`, verifies `projects`, `agent_sessions`, `agent_messages`, `memory_entries`, `cost_tracking`, `audit_log`, pgvector/pgcrypto, and four LangGraph checkpoint tables. `scripts\verify-phase1-runtime.ps1` fails closed if the proof cannot be generated; `scripts\verify-phase1.ps1` fails closed if the script or gitignore guard disappears.
- PostgreSQL restore proof: `scripts\restore-postgres-phase1-proof.ps1` creates an isolated `superbrain_restore_probe_*` database, restores the schema-only dump, verifies six app tables, pgvector/pgcrypto, and four LangGraph checkpoint tables, then drops the probe database unless explicitly run with `-KeepDatabase`; runtime and static verifiers now fail closed if the restore drill disappears.
- Redis persistence proof: `scripts\verify-redis-persistence-phase1.ps1` verifies `appendonly yes`, writes a TTL proof key, runs `docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml up -d --force-recreate redis`, confirms the value survives the Redis recreate, and deletes the proof key. Runtime and static verifiers now fail closed if Redis AOF persistence or the proof script disappears.
- Runtime secret redaction proof: Agent API redacts prompt, memory, task-description, and audit-detail strings using `services/agent-api/app/security.py`. Runtime verifier posts a fake secret prompt, proves the raw fake secret is absent from `agent_messages` and `memory_entries`, and proves the masked marker is present instead.
- Phase transition gate proof: `docs/phase-transition-gate.json` marks Phase 1 as locally transition-ready to Phase 2 while explicitly keeping live-secret and release gates closed. `scripts\verify-phase-transition-gate.py`, `scripts\verify-phase1.ps1`, `scripts\verify-phase1-runtime.ps1`, and `scripts\verify-hosted-staging.ps1` passed after the gate was added.
- SSE replay proof: `/api/v1/session/{session_id}/stream` now emits `id:` fields, stores the last 50 session events in Redis, and honors `Last-Event-ID`. Runtime and hosted verifiers assert original stream IDs and replayed `token`/`done` events with `replay=true`.
- LangGraph hard-stop audit proof: `/api/v1/orchestrator/dry-run` now persists `langgraph_dry_run_completed` or `langgraph_dry_run_stopped` audit rows. Runtime and hosted verifiers prove forbidden dry-runs stop at `hard_stop`, increment retry counters, remain `live_provider_calls=false`, are recoverable from PostgreSQL checkpoints, and are visible in `/api/v1/audit/recent`.
- LangGraph Orchestrator replay proof: `/api/v1/orchestrator/dry-run/stream` now emits buffered SSE ids and supports `Last-Event-ID` replay for completed and hard-stopped deterministic graph streams without re-running live providers.
- LLM Gateway runtime proof: `docker-compose.dev.yml` now runs a separate `llm-gateway` app container on internal port `4000`, Nginx routes `/llm/` to it, Agent API health includes `llm_gateway`, and runtime/hosted verifiers prove OpenAI-compatible dry-run completions with zero cost and no live provider calls.
- LLM Gateway orchestration proof: LangGraph dry-runs now route the Planner executor through the gateway contract, persist a `llm_gateway_request` audit row with the LangGraph trace id, and expose the gateway evidence through normal run JSON plus Orchestrator SSE/replay streams.
- LLM Gateway routing-resolver proof: `POST /llm/api/v1/routing/resolve` deterministically resolves an agent/task route, returns selected model, fallback chain, max output tokens, budget reason, and `live_provider_calls=false`; runtime and hosted verifiers assert planner routing selects `claude-sonnet-4-6` and remains configured-only.
- Orchestrator routing-consumption proof: LangGraph `agent_executor` now calls `/api/v1/routing/resolve` before `/v1/chat/completions`, stores the route under `llm_gateway_calls[0].routing`, and runtime/hosted verifiers assert selected model, configured-only mode, and no live provider calls.
- LLM Provider snapshot proof: `GET /llm/api/v1/providers/status` exposes configured-only provider status, model membership, backoff `[30,60,120,300]`, reset `900`, and `live_provider_calls=false`; runtime and hosted verifiers assert Anthropic/OpenAI fallback visibility without claiming live provider health checks.
- LLM Routing UI proof: frontend renders `Routing Resolver`, `Selected Model`, `Route Reason`, and the fallback chain inside the `LLM Gateway` panel; runtime and hosted verifiers assert this panel text from the served HTML.
- LLM Provider UI proof: frontend renders `Provider Health`, selected provider, provider chain, provider models, backoff, reset, and the no-live-health-check non-claim inside the `LLM Gateway` panel.
- LLM Gateway streaming contract proof: `GET /llm/api/v1/streaming/contract` exposes the deterministic OpenAI-compatible SSE contract (`text/event-stream`, `data: {chat.completion.chunk}`, terminal `data: [DONE]`, `live_provider_calls=false`). Runtime and hosted verifiers POST `/llm/v1/chat/completions` with `stream=true` and assert chunk frames, `[DONE]`, zero live provider calls, and audit persistence.
- LLM Gateway streaming UI proof: frontend renders `Streaming Contract` and the configured stream frames inside the `LLM Gateway` panel, so the LiteLLM/agent streaming protocol is visible instead of implicit.
- LangGraph streaming-consumption proof: `agent_executor` now calls the LLM Gateway with `stream=true`, parses OpenAI-compatible SSE chunks, records `streaming_used=true`, `streaming_protocol=openai_compatible_sse`, `stream_chunk_count>=1`, `stream_done_seen=true`, and keeps `live_provider_calls=false`. Runtime and hosted verifiers assert these fields in direct dry-run output, Orchestrator SSE output, replay output, and audit visibility.
- LangGraph LLM routing policy deny hard-stop proof: `agent_executor` now routes through `route_after_agent_executor`; a forced `deny_sensitive_cache` policy response returns `policy_blocked`, sets `hard_stop_reason=llm_routing_policy_rejected`, preserves `llm_routing_policy_sensitive_cache_blocked` evidence, and reaches `hard_stop` without `task_assignments[]` or `mcp_tool_calls[]`. Runtime and hosted verifiers assert direct output plus PostgreSQL checkpoint recovery.
- Phase 2 runtime graph start proof: `POST /api/v1/phase2/runtime/start` wraps the LangGraph path in contract `phase2-runtime-v1`, stores `phase2_runtime_graph_started` in state, checkpoint, audit log, and frontend dashboard, while leaving live-provider, live-MCP-write, and production-deploy gates closed.
- LangGraph memory-context injection proof: `agent_executor` now calls `search_memory()` before the LLM Gateway stream, injects selected memory into the deterministic dry-run system context, enforces `MEMORY_CONTEXT_BUDGET_PERCENT_MAX = 30`, records `memory_context`, `memory_context_budget`, `memory_context_injected=true`, and keeps usage under the context-token budget. Runtime and hosted verifiers assert direct dry-run output plus Orchestrator SSE/replay output.
- LangGraph memory-updater persistence proof: `memory_updater` now writes a redacted `langgraph memory update` row through the shared Memory service after successful Orchestrator execution, returns `memory_update_id`, and adds `memory_update_persisted` evidence. Runtime and hosted verifiers search `/api/v1/memory/search` for the persisted update id/text and assert the evidence in direct dry-run, SSE stream, and replay output.
- LangGraph typed task-assignment proof: `agent_executor` now creates a fail-closed `TaskAssignment`, enqueues it through the shared Redis `tasks:agent:queue`, waits for the Agent Worker result, records `task_assignments[]`, and adds `task_assignment_completed` evidence. Runtime and hosted verifiers assert the `TaskAssignment` contract, `status=completed`, `done_validation.logged=true`, required blocked actions such as `push_main`, public task visibility, and SSE/replay evidence.
- LangGraph MCP safe-tool proof: `agent_executor` now calls the MCP Gateway through a read-only filesystem `McpToolRequest` envelope scoped to `/tmp/agent-workspace/read-only`, records `mcp_tool_calls[]`, and adds `mcp_tool_success` evidence. Runtime and hosted verifiers assert `status=success`, `evidence_ref=mcp_safe_envelope`, `audit_persisted=true`, audit-feed visibility, and SSE/replay evidence.
- Agent profile runtime contract proof: `services/agent-api/app/models.py` now defines deterministic Planner/Coder/Tester/DevOps profiles with primary/fallback models, max output tokens, max execution seconds, max retries, allowed tools, blocked actions, graceful-degradation policy, and human-review gates. `GET /api/v1/agents/profiles`, `GET /api/v1/agents/status`, `GET /api/v1/models/capabilities`, the frontend dashboard, runtime verifier, and hosted verifier now prove the profile registry; DevOps also has a matching LLM Gateway route.
- Agent profile policy-enforcement proof: `services/agent-api/app/tasks.py` now derives task-policy gates from `AGENT_PROFILES`; runtime and hosted verifiers prove a valid scoped Coder task is accepted, a Coder write task without `write_scope` is rejected, and a Planner task requesting `github_mcp` is rejected with `task_policy_violation` plus `profile does not allow tools`. The task policy manifest and frontend dashboard expose `profile_contract_version=agent-profiles-v1` and per-agent `profile_gates`.
- DevOps workflow-dispatch contract proof: Agent API exposes `GET /api/v1/devops/workflow-dispatch/plan` and `POST /api/v1/devops/workflow-dispatch/validate`. Runtime and hosted verifiers prove staging dry-run dispatch contracts return `status=ready`, `live_github_call=false`, exact GitHub Actions API method/endpoint/payload/permission metadata, production dispatch without `human_review_approved=true` returns HTTP `403 workflow_dispatch_blocked`, and both ready/blocked contracts are persisted as `devops_workflow_dispatch_contract` audit events. - MCP GitHub Branch/PR dry-run contract proof: MCP Gateway now supports `toolset=github` + `capability=plan_branch_pr` as a no-mutation contract. - GitHub Branch/PR Contract UI proof: MCP Gateway now exposes `GET /mcp/api/v1/github/branch-pr/contract` with `contract_version=github-branch-pr-plan-v1`, `mode=dry_run_contract_only`, `live_github_call=false`, `allowed_branch_pattern=feature/agent-*`, forbidden capabilities, accepted/blocked evidence refs, payload templates, and explicit non-claims. - **SECURITY-INVALIDATED / SUPERSEDED — historical Auth GitHub/JWT/Refresh summary:** The old summary reported the `auth-github-jwt-refresh-v1` routes, no claimed live GitHub exchange, 900-second access and 604800-second refresh TTLs, secure cookie flags, refresh rotation/blacklist/replay rejection, logout revocation, and persisted `auth_refresh_rotated`, `auth_refresh_reuse_blocked`, and `auth_logout_revoked` events.
